It exists for the\n 682|+disposition of audit finding ``EXP002D1-S2-F2``. With the shipped 900 s per-cell\n 683|+bound, a notebook leg that is slow inside the full suite is *killed* at 900 s, so\n 684|+its true in-suite duration is never observed (the data are censored). To size a\n 685|+bound from evidence (Testing Standards section 7 item 2) the slow leg has to be\n 686|+allowed to finish once, while the host is observed.\n 687|+\n 688|+Loading this plugin (``-p diag_nb_timeout`` with ``EVIDENCE/exp002-d1-s3`` on\n 689|+``PYTHONPATH``) wraps ``nbclient.NotebookClient.__init__`` in the pytest process\n 690|+so that every notebook client is built with a four-hour per-cell timeout. It does\n 691|+nothing else: no test is selected, skipped, reordered or altered, no assertion is\n 692|+touched, and ``tests/test_notebooks.py`` on disk is unchanged. The gate runs of\n 693|+this session do **not** load it.\n 694|+\n 695|+A run that loads this plugin is recorded as a *diagnostic* run and is never\n 696|+reported as the required full-suite gate.\n 697|+\"\"\"\n 698|+\n 699|+from __future__ import annotations\n 700|+\n 701|+from typing import Any\n 702|+\n 703|+import nbclient\n 704|+\n 705|+DIAGNOSTIC_CELL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 14400\n 706|+\n 707|+_original_init: Any = nbclient.NotebookClient.__init__\n 708|+\n 709|+\n 710|+def _init_with_diagnostic_timeout(self: Any, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> None:\n 711|+    \"\"\"Build a ``NotebookClient`` with the diagnostic per-cell timeout.\n 712|+\n 713|+    Args:\n 714|+        self: The client being constructed.\n 715|+        *args: Positional arguments passed through unchanged.\n 716|+        **kwargs: Keyword arguments passed through, with ``timeout`` replaced.\n 717|+    \"\"\"\n 718|+    kwargs[\"timeout\"] = DIAGNOSTIC_CELL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS\n 719|+    _original_init(self, *args, **kwargs)\n 720|+\n 721|+\n 722|+nbclient.NotebookClient.__init__ = _init_with_diagnostic_timeout  # type: ignore[method-assign]\n 723|diff --git a/EVIDENCE/exp002-d1-s3/diagnostic_suite_run.sh b/EVIDENCE/exp002-d1-s3/diagnostic_suite_run.sh\n 724|new file mode 100644\n 725|index 00000000..6a056df2\n 726|--- /dev/null\n 727|+++ b/EVIDENCE/exp002-d1-s3/diagnostic_suite_run.sh\n 728|@@ -0,0 +1,73 @@\n 729|+#!/usr/bin/env bash\n 730|+# EXP-002 D1 S3 - observed pytest run (finding EXP002D1-S2-F2).\n 731|+#\n 732|+#   bash EVIDENCE/exp002-d1-s3/diagnostic_suite_run.sh <label> [--lift-notebook-timeout] <pytest args...>\n 733|+#\n 734|+# Starts the out-of-process sampler (suite_sampler.py), then runs pytest with the\n 735|+# required instrumentation (-o faulthandler_timeout=300 --durations=20,\n 736|+# --basetemp=.pytest_basetemp, Git Bash first on PATH) and a UTC timestamp on\n 737|+# every console line. With --lift-notebook-timeout the diagnostic plugin\n 738|+# diag_nb_timeout.py is loaded so a slow notebook leg is measured instead of\n 739|+# killed at 900 s; a run that does so is a DIAGNOSTIC run, never the gate.\n 740|+#\n 741|+# Everything this script records goes OUTSIDE the repository, to\n 742|+# ~/prin-s3-scratch/<label>/, so it cannot change the tree it is measuring:\n 743|+#   console.log   timestamped pytest console output\n 744|+#   samples.jsonl sampler output (one JSON object per 30 s)\n 745|+#   run.txt       commands, UTC start/end, git HEAD, pytest exit status\n 746|+# The one in-repository write is pytest's own --basetemp=.pytest_basetemp, which\n 747|+# AGENTS.md mandates by that exact name and which is gitignored. run.txt records\n 748|+# the tracked-change and untracked-file counts before and after the run, so a\n 749|+# change to the tree would show.\n 750|+set -u\n 751|+LABEL=\"${1:?usage: diagnostic_suite_run.sh <label> [--lift-notebook-timeout] <pytest args...>}\"\n 752|+shift\n 753|+# A label is a directory name under ~/prin-s3-scratch, so it must not be able to\n 754|+# climb out of it: letters, digits, '_' and '-', not starting with '-' (no dots).\n 755|+[[ \"$LABEL\" =~ ^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_-]*$ ]] || { echo \"label must match [A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_-]*\"; exit 2; }\n 756|+LIFT=0\n 757|+if [ \"${1:-}\" = \"--lift-notebook-timeout\" ]; then LIFT=1; shift; fi\n 758|+\n 759|+cd \"$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)\" || exit 2\n 760|+OUT=\"$HOME/prin-s3-scratch/$LABEL\"\n 761|+mkdir -p \"$OUT\"\n 762|+export PATH=\"/c/Program Files/Git/bin:$PATH\"\n 763|+PY=.venv/Scripts/python.exe\n 764|+PLUGIN=()\n 765|+if [ \"$LIFT\" -eq 1 ]; then\n 766|+  export PYTHONPATH=\"$PWD/EVIDENCE/exp002-d1-s3${PYTHONPATH:+:$PYTHONPATH}\"\n 767|+  PLUGIN=(-p diag_nb_timeout)\n 768|+fi\n 769|+\n 770|+{\n 771|+  echo \"label: $LABEL\"\n 772|+  echo \"git HEAD: $(git rev-parse HEAD)\"\n 773|+  echo \"tracked changes vs HEAD: $(git status --porcelain --untracked-files=no | wc -l)\"\n 774|+  echo \"untracked files: $(git status --porcelain | grep -c '^??')\"\n 775|+  echo \"host: $(uname -srm)   $($PY -V 2>&1)   logical CPUs: $(nproc 2>/dev/null)\"\n 776|+  echo \"diagnostic notebook-timeout plugin loaded: $LIFT\"\n 777|+  echo \"pytest args: $*\"\n 778|+  echo \"started: $(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)\"\n 779|+} > \"$OUT/run.txt\"\n 780|+\n 781|+$PY -I EVIDENCE/exp002-d1-s3/suite_sampler.py --period 30 > \"$OUT/samples.jsonl\" 2> \"$OUT/sampler.err\" &\n 782|+SAMPLER=$!\n 783|+\n 784|+$PY -m pytest \"$@\" --basetemp=.pytest_basetemp -o faulthandler_timeout=300 --durations=20 ${PLUGIN[@]+\"${PLUGIN[@]}\"} 2>&1 \\\n 785|+  | awk '{print strftime(\"%H:%M:%SZ\", systime(), 1), $0; fflush()}' > \"$OUT/console.log\"\n 786|+STATUS=${PIPESTATUS[0]}\n 787|+\n 788|+# The sampler stops by itself once pytest has been gone for two samples (about\n 789|+# 60 s). Allow it 150 s, then stop it, so this script can never hang on it.\n 790|+for _ in $(seq 1 30); do kill -0 \"$SAMPLER\" 2>/dev/null || break; sleep 5; done\n 791|+if kill -0 \"$SAMPLER\" 2>/dev/null; then\n 792|+  kill \"$SAMPLER\" 2>/dev/null\n 793|+  echo \"sampler did not exit by itself within 150 s and was stopped\" >> \"$OUT/run.txt\"\n 794|+fi\n 795|+wait \"$SAMPLER\" 2>/dev/null\n 796|+{\n 797|+  echo \"pytest exit status: $STATUS\"\n 798|+  echo \"ended: $(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)\"\n 799|+  echo \"git HEAD at end: $(git rev-parse HEAD)   tracked changes: $(git status --porcelain --untracked-files=no | wc -l)   untracked files: $(git status --porcelain | grep -c '^??')\"\n 800|+} >> \"$OUT/run.txt\"\n 801|+exit \"$STATUS\"\n 802|diff --git a/EVIDENCE/exp002-d1-s3/docstring-only-check.txt b/EVIDENCE/exp002-d1-s3/docstring-only-check.txt\n 803|new file mode 100644\n 804|index 00000000..8deb7886\n 805|--- /dev/null\n 806|+++ b/EVIDENCE/exp002-d1-s3/docstring-only-check.txt\n 807|@@ -0,0 +1,47 @@\n 808|+# EXP002D1-S2-F5 / -F7 - the three docstring-only commits changed no executable code\n 809|+# date (UTC): 2026-10-16T03:12:31Z\n 810|+# git HEAD: 33923be51ea4dfefc62ef2c3f97a3158dd85c7ba\n 811|+# instrument: EVIDENCE/exp002-d1-s3/docstring_only_check.py (reads each file at both revisions from git as UTF-8 bytes, drops the module docstring, compares ast.dump)\n 812|+# Each check compares a commit with ITS OWN PARENT. The cause is CPU starvation by processes outside PRIN, not memory and not the\n1055|+notebook.** [`analysis-observed-run-timeline.txt`](analysis-observed-run-timeline.txt)\n1056|+puts the host's CPU next to the CPU seconds per second that pytest received:\n1057|+\n1058|+- from 03:21Z to 03:32:55Z pytest received a median of **2.8 CPU-s/s** (range\n1059|+  0.8–4.7) while the host varied between 15% and 89% CPU;\n1060|+- from 03:35Z, when the first `tests/test_exp002_*` tests have started (the host\n1061|+  first reads 93.5% at 03:33:47Z), **all 102 samples to 06:00Z show the host at\n1062|+  98–100%** and pytest receiving a median of **0.01 CPU-s/s** (maximum 0.23), a\n1063|+  drop of about 280×, for 2 h 27 m;\n1064|+- the CPU is used by `System`, `mc-fw-host.exe`, `Devin.exe` and `svchost.exe`\n1065|+  throughout, and from 03:44Z (in the top three from 03:56Z) also by `rg.exe`: at 04:37Z **134 concurrent\n1066|+  `rg.exe --files --hidden --case-sensitive --no-require-git …` processes, all\n1067|+  children of one `Devin.exe` Node utility process, with 37 distinct `-g` patterns\n1068|+  (`PLAN.md`, `.cursorrules`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `AGENT.md`, …) and 2,093\n1069|+  cumulative CPU-seconds** ([`rg-storm-snapshot-during-suite.txt`](rg-storm-snapshot-during-suite.txt));\n1070|+- during the notebook kernels ([`analysis-observed-run-kernels.txt`](analysis-observed-run-kernels.txt))\n1071|+  the kernel received **0.17–0.33 CPU-s per wall-second**, `rg.exe` alone used\n1072|+  202–8,799 CPU-seconds in the same windows, host available memory never fell\n1073|+  below **5.5 GB**, and hard-fault paging averaged **at most 26 pages/s**: not\n1074|+  memory pressure;\n1075|+- when pytest exited, the host fell from 100% to 33% within about a minute and\n1076|+  `rg.exe` processes fell from 116 to 2.\n1077|+\n1078|+**4. A smaller group reproduces it.** `contention_experiment.sh` measured one\n1079|+notebook twice, changing only the company it kept\n1080|+([`analysis-paired-comparison.txt`](analysis-paired-comparison.txt)):\n1081|+\n1082|+| | wall | kernel CPU | CPU / wall | host CPU |\n1083|+|---|---|---|---|---|\n1084|+| control: notebook 02 alone, quiet host | 80.7 s | 465.7 s | 5.77 | 73.5% |\n1085|+| treatment: notebook 02 while only `tests/test_exp002_driver.py` and `tests/test_exp002_e4_analysis.py` run | **3484.0 s (43.2×)** | 1116.9 s (2.4×) | 0.32 | 99.9% |\n1086|+\n1087|+The training cell alone went from 35.7 s to 1,926 s (54×). The 499-test load\n1088|+generator ran 30 m 37 s, and the host stayed at ~100% with 87 → 29 → 64 `rg.exe`\n1089|+processes for more than 28 minutes after it had exited\n1090|+([`analysis-contention-timeline.txt`](analysis-contention-timeline.txt)).\n1091|+\n1092|+## What is measured and what is not\n1093|+\n1094|+- **Measured:** the notebook's standalone cost; its in-suite and beside-the-exp002-tests\n1095|+  durations; the CPU share it received; which processes were using the CPU; that\n1096|+  memory and paging were not limiting; that the saturation began when the\n1097|+  `test_exp002_*` group started in both the observed run and the experiment, and\n1098|+  that the experiment's slowdown (43×) exceeds the full-suite's (42×).\n1099|+- **Not established:** what starts the `rg.exe` scans or why `System` and\n1100|+  `mc-fw-host.exe` are busy. The timing says the file-heavy `test_exp002_*` tests\n1101|+  trigger them (workspace changes under the in-repository `.pytest_basetemp`, 37,591\n1102|+  files at the time of the snapshot), but no process was stopped to test that, and\n1103|+  nothing here proves the IDE's scans, rather than another process, are what\n1104|+  starve the kernel. These are the maintainer's processes and were observed, not\n1105|+  killed. Why the contention drained within a minute of the end of the full suite\n1106|+  but persisted for over 28 minutes after the experiment's load generator exited\n1107|+  is not explained either.\n1108|+- **Not PRIN code.** Nothing in the notebooks, the library or the tests is\n1109|+  implicated by this evidence.\n1110|+\n1111|+## Disposition (route (a))\n1112|+\n1113|+Testing Standards §7 item 2 sizes a finite bound from cited completed evidence for\n1114|+that workload plus substantial margin for host variability. The shipped 900 s bound\n1115|+was sized from the notebooks' stated ~40 s budget and is below every contended\n1116|+duration recorded. `tests/test_notebooks.py` now carries a per-notebook bound of\n1117|+**3 × the largest completed contended duration, rounded up to a multiple of 900 s**:\n1118|+\n1119|+| notebook | largest completed contended duration | bound |\n1120|+|---|---|---|\n1121|+| 01_oscillosim_quickstart | 800.07 s (S2, full suite) | 2,700 s |\n1122|+| 02_clevr_n_binding | 3,484.0 s (S3 experiment) | 10,800 s |\n1123|+| 03_custom_coupling | 408.96 s (S1, full suite) | 1,800 s |\n1124|+| 04_torch_bridge | 389.81 s (S1, full suite) | 1,800 s |\n1125|+\n1126|+No test was deselected, skipped, xfailed or shortened, and no assertion changed;\n1127|+the execution test still fails on any cell error output or timeout. The bound is\n1128|+now a ceiling for a leg that is starved but progressing, not a performance check\n1129|+(the quiet-host durations are 37–73 s). Two new fast tests pin the relationship\n1130|+so a bound cannot be lowered, or a slower worst case recorded, without the other\n1131|+moving: [`test-red-before-fix.txt`](test-red-before-fix.txt) (4 failed with the old\n1132|+900 s bounds) and [`test-green-after-fix.txt`](test-green-after-fix.txt)\n1133|+(33 passed, 4 slow executing tests still collected).\n1134|+\n1135|+## Files\n1136|+\n1137|+The runner writes the console output as `console.log` under `~/prin-s3-scratch/`; the copies kept here are named `console.txt` because the repository ignores `*.log`.\n1138|+\n1139|+| File | What it is |\n1140|+|---|---|\n1141|+| [`alone-01_oscillosim_quickstart.json`](alone-01_oscillosim_quickstart.json), [`alone-02_clevr_n_binding.json`](alone-02_clevr_n_binding.json), [`alone-03_custom_coupling.json`](alone-03_custom_coupling.json), [`alone-04_torch_bridge.json`](alone-04_torch_bridge.json) | Standalone baseline: `notebook_timing.py` on each notebook, one after another, quiet host, no pytest. |\n1142|+| [`diagnostic-full-suite/run.txt`](diagnostic-full-suite/run.txt) | The observed run's header and footer: git HEAD `2f796ddf`, 0 tracked changes and 0 untracked files before and after, plugin loaded, pytest args, start/end UTC, exit status 0. |\n1143|+| [`diagnostic-full-suite/console.txt`](diagnostic-full-suite/console.txt) | The full pytest console output with a UTC timestamp on every line, including the `--durations` table and the final summary. |\n1144|+| [`diagnostic-full-suite/samples.jsonl`](diagnostic-full-suite/samples.jsonl) | The sampler's 132 samples (30 s cadence). |\n1145|+| [`rg-storm-snapshot-during-suite.txt`](rg-storm-snapshot-during-suite.txt) | A read-only snapshot of the 134 `rg.exe` processes at 04:37Z: parent, count, cumulative CPU, `-g` patterns. @@\n3557|+\"\"\"Print the control-versus-treatment result of the F2 contention experiment.\n3558|+\n3559|+Reads the two ``notebook_timing.py`` records written by\n3560|+``contention_experiment.sh`` from their fixed locations in this bundle\n3561|+(``f2/contention-experiment/control.json`` and ``treatment.json``) and prints, side\n3562|+by side, what changed between them: the same notebook, run alone on a quiet host\n3563|+versus run while only the ``tests/test_exp002_*`` group executed beside it.\n3564|+\n3565|+It takes no arguments and reads no caller-supplied path::\n3566|+\n3567|+    .venv/Scripts/python.exe -I EVIDENCE/exp002-d1-s3/paired_comparison.py\n3568|+\n3569|+Exit status is 0 when both records exist and both notebooks completed.\n3570|+\"\"\"\n3571|+\n3572|+from __future__ import annotations\n3573|+\n3574|+import json\n3575|+import sys\n3576|+from pathlib import Path\n3577|+from typing import Any\n3578|+\n3579|+EXPERIMENT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent / \"f2\" / \"contention-experiment\"\n3580|+\n3581|+\n3582|+def load(name: str) -> dict[str, Any]:\n3583|+    \"\"\"Read one experiment record.\n3584|+\n3585|+    Args:\n3586|+        name: ``control`` or ``treatment``.\n3587|+\n3588|+    Returns:\n3589|+        The parsed ``notebook_timing.py`` record.\n3590|+    \"\"\"\n3591|+    record: dict[str, Any] = json.loads(\n3592|+        (EXPERIMENT / f\"{name}.json\").read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n3593|+    )\n3594|+    return record\n3595|+\n3596|+\n3597|+def slowest_cell(record: dict[str, Any]) -> tuple[int, float]:\n3598|+    \"\"\"Return the slowest code cell's index and wall seconds.\n3599|+\n3600|+    Args:\n3601|+        record: A ``notebook_timing.py`` record.\n3602|+\n3603|+    Returns:\n3604|+        ``(cell index, seconds)``.\n3605|+    \"\"\"\n3606|+    cells = [(c[\"cell\"], c[\"wall_seconds\"] or 0.0) for c in record[\"cells\"]]\n3607|+    return max(cells, key=lambda item: item[1])\n3608|+\n3609|+\n3610|+def main() -> int:\n3611|+    \"\"\"Print the comparison table.\n3612|+\n3613|+    Returns:\n3614|+        The process exit status.\n3615|+    \"\"\"\n3616|+    control, treatment = load(\"control\"), load(\"treatment\")\n3617|+    rows = (\n3618|+        (\"outcome\", control[\"outcome\"], treatment[\"outcome\"]),\n3619|+        (\"wall seconds\", control[\"wall_seconds\"], treatment[\"wall_seconds\"]),\n3620|+        (\n3621|+            \"kernel CPU seconds\",\n3622|+            control[\"kernel_cpu_seconds_last_sample\"],\n3623|+            treatment[\"kernel_cpu_seconds_last_sample\"],\n3624|+        ),\n3625|+        (\n3626|+            \"kernel CPU / wall\",\n3627|+            control[\"kernel_cpu_over_wall\"],\n3628|+            treatment[\"kernel_cpu_over_wall\"],\n3629|+        ),\n3630|+        (\n3631|+            \"host CPU % (mean of samples)\",\n3632|+            control[\"host_cpu_percent_mean\"],\n3633|+            treatment[\"host_cpu_percent_mean\"],\n3634|+        ),\n3635|+        (\n3636|+            \"host available memory, MB (min)\",\n3637|+            control[\"host_available_mb_min\"],\n3638|+            treatment[\"host_available_mb_min\"],\n3639|+        ),\n3640|+        (\n3641|+            \"kernel peak working set, MB\",\n3642|+            control[\"kernel_peak_working_set_mb\"],\n3643|+ slowest_cell(treatment),\n3649|+        ),\n3650|+    )\n3651|+    print(f\"notebook: {control['notebook']}\")\n3652|+    print(f\"{'':36s}{'control (quiet host)':>24s}{'treatment (exp002 tests)':>26s}\")\n3653|+    for label, left, right in rows:\n3654|+        print(f\"{label:36s}{left!s:>24s}{right!s:>26s}\")\n3655|+    ratio = treatment[\"wall_seconds\"] / control[\"wall_seconds\"]\n3656|+    cell_ratio = slowest_cell(treatment)[1] / slowest_cell(control)[1]\n3657|+    print()\n3658|+    print(f\"treatment / control wall time        : {ratio:.1f}x\")\n3659|+    print(f\"treatment / control slowest-cell time: {cell_ratio:.1f}x\")\n3660|+    cpu_ratio = (\n3661|+        treatment[\"kernel_cpu_seconds_last_sample\"]\n3662|+        / control[\"kernel_cpu_seconds_last_sample\"]\n3663|+    )\n3664|+    print(f\"treatment / control kernel CPU time  : {cpu_ratio:.1f}x\")\n3665|+    print(\"  (more CPU for the same work: consistent @@\n3679|+\"\"\"Show that a change to ``figure_generation.py`` moved no output byte.\n3680|+\n3681|+Backs the closure of audit finding ``EXP002D1-S2-F6``. Restoring the reference's\n3682|+fig13 per-series ``label=`` is meant to be byte-immaterial, because fig13 draws\n3683|+no legend. S2 reported the two generators equal under the registered\n3684|+*normalized* comparison and equal at artist level, but a change that was\n3685|+\"immaterial\" has to be shown to stay immaterial, and the strongest available\n3686|+statement is the plain one: render every figure with the module before and after\n3687|+the change, and compare the **raw** bytes.\n3688|+\n3689|+For each of the two revisions this renders all fourteen historical figures\n3690|+(``generate_all_figures``) from the archived PRINet 3.0 benchmark JSON into a\n3691|+temporary directory and records the SHA-256 of every ``.pdf`` and ``.png``. The\n3692|+two digest tables must be identical. PRIN's raw output is deterministic (EXP-002\n3693|+hypothesis H4), so any difference is a real change in the output, not noise.\n3694|+\n3695|+Each revision's module source is read with ``git show`` as bytes (UTF-8) and\n3696|+imported from a temporary file under a distinct module name, so the two variants\n3697|+coexist in one process without editing the working tree. ``worktree`` as the\n3698|+after-revision means the file as it currently is on disk.\n3699|+\n3700|+Revisions must be hexadecimal commit ids (or the word ``worktree`` for the\n3701|+after-revision only); no caller-supplied string becomes a filesystem path.\n3702|+\n3703|+Run from the repository root::\n3704|+\n3705|+    .venv/Scripts/python.exe -I EVIDENCE/exp002-d1-s3/raw_byte_identity.py \\\n3706|+        --before <rev> [--after <rev>|worktree]\n3707|+\n3708|+Exit status is 0 when the digest tables are identical (and non-empty), 1 when\n3709|+any file differs, 2 on a usage error.\n3710|+\"\"\"\n3711|+\n3712|+from __future__ import annotations\n3713|+\n3714|+import argparse\n3715|+import hashlib\n3716|+import importlib.util\n3717|+import re\n3718|+import shutil\n3719|+import subprocess  # nosec B404 - fixed argv, no shell\n3720|+import sys\n3721|+import tempfile\n3722|+from pathlib import Path\n3723|+from types import ModuleType\n3724|+from typing import Any\n3725|+\n3726|+ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]\n3727|+MODULE_REL = \"python/prin/reporting/figure_generation.py\"\n3728|+MODULE_PATH = ROOT / \"python\" / \"prin\" / \"reporting\" / \"figure_generation.py\"\n3729|+RESULTS = (\n3730|+    ROOT\n3731|+    / \"DOCS\"\n3732|+    / \"archive and reference from PRINet 3.0\"\n3733|+    / \"PRINet-3.0.0-main\"\n3734|+    / \"benchmarks\"\n3735|+    / \"results\"\n3736|+)\n3737|+REVISION = re.compile(r\"[0-9a-f]{7,40}\")\n3738|+\n3739|+#: ``git`` resolved once to an absolute path (Coding Standards section 6.1:\n3740|+#: \"absolute executables\"), as ``tools/check_deviation_ledger.py`` does.\n3741|+_GIT = shutil.which(\"git\")\n3742|+if _GIT is None:\n3743|+    raise SystemExit(\"git was not found on PATH\")\n3744|+GIT: str = _GIT\n3745|+\n3746|+\n3747|+def module_source(revision: str) -> bytes:\n3748|+    \"\"\"Return the module's source bytes at a revision (or on disk).\n3749|+\n3750|+    Args:\n3751|+        revision: A hexadecimal commit id, or ``worktree``.\n3752|+\n3753|+    Returns:\n3754|+        The file's bytes.\n3755|+\n3756|+    Raises:\n3757|+        SystemExit: If the revision is malformed.\n3758|+    \"\"\"\n3759|+    if revision == \"worktree\":\n3760|+        return MODULE_PATH.read_bytes()\n3761|+    if not REVISION.fullmatch(revision):\n3762|+        raise SystemExit(f\"not a hexadecimal revision: {revision!r}\")\n3763|+    return subprocess.run(  # noqa: S603  # nosec B603 - absolute git, fixed argv, no shell\n3764|+        [GIT, \"show\", f\"{revision}:{MODULE_REL}\"],\n3765|+        cwd=ROOT,\n3766|+        capture_output=True,\n3767|+        check=True,\n3768|+    ).stdout\n3769|+\n3770|+\n3771|+def load_variant(source: bytes, name: str, scratch: Path) -> ModuleType:\n3772|+    \"\"\"Import a copy of the module from ``source`` under the name ``name``.\n3773|+\n3774|+    Args:\n3775|+        source: The module's source bytes.\n3776|+        name: A unique module name.\n3777|+        scratch: A directory the copy may be written to.\n3778|+\n3779|+    Returns:\n3780|+        The imported module.\n3781|+    \"\"\"\n3782|+    path = scratch / f\"{name}.py\"\n3783|+    path.write_bytes(source)\n3784|+    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(name, path)\n3785|+    if spec is None or spec.loader is None:\n3786|+        raise SystemExit(f\"cannot import the {name} variant\")\n3787|+    module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)\n3788|+    sys.modules[name] = module\n3789|+    spec.loader.exec_module(module)\n3790|+    return module\n3791|+\n3792|+\n3793|+def digests(module: Any, output: Path) -> dict[str, str]:\n3794|+    \"\"\"Render every figure with ``module`` and hash the raw output files.\n3795|+\n3796|+    Args:\n3797|+        module: A loaded variant of ``figure_generation``.\n3798|+        output: Directory to render into (made an allowed output root).\n3799|+\n3800|+    Returns:\n3801|+        A mapping from file name to the SHA-256 of its raw bytes.\n3802|+    \"\"\"\n3803|+    output.mkdir(parents=True)\n3804|+    module.ALLOWED_OUTPUT_ROOTS = (output.resolve(),)\n3805|+    module.generate_all_figures(RESULTS, output)\n3806|+    return {\n3807|+        path.name: hashlib.sha256(path.read_bytes()).hexdigest()\n3808|+        for path in sorted(output.iterdir())\n3809|+    }\n3810|+\n3811|+\n3812|+def main(argv: list[str]) -> int:\n3813|+    \"\"\"Compare the raw digest tables of two revisions.\n3814|+\n3815|+    Args:\n3816|+        argv: Command-line arguments without the program name.\n3817|+\n3818|+    Returns:\n3819|+        The process exit status.\n3820|+    \"\"\"\n3821|+    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.split(\"\\n\\n\", maxsplit=1)[0])\n3822|+    parser.add_argument(\"--before\", required=True)\n3823|+    parser.add_argument(\"--after\", default=\"worktree\")\n3824|+    args = parser.parse_args(argv)\n3825|+    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix=\"prin-s3-rawid-\") as scratch_name:\n3826|+        scratch = Path(scratch_name)\n3827|+        before = digests(\n3828|+            load_variant(module_source(args.before), \"figgen_before\", scratch),\n3829|+            scratch / \"before\",\n3830|+        )\n3831|+        after = digests(\n3832|+            load_variant(module_source(args.after), \"figgen_after\", scratch),\n3833|+            scratch / \"after\",\n3834|+        )\n3835|+    differing = sorted(\n3836|+        n for n in before.keys() | after.keys() if before.get(n) != after.get(n)\n3837|+    )\n3838|+    print(f\"before: {args.before}   after: {args.after}\")\n3839|+    print(f\"files rendered: before {len(before)}, after {len(after)}\")\n3840|+    print(f\"raw-byte identical: {len(before) - len(differing)}\")\n3841|+    print(f\"raw-byte differing: {len(differing)} {differing or ''}\")\n3842|+    for name in sorted(before):\n3843|+        print(f\"  {before[name]}  {name}\")\n3844|+    return 0 if before and not differing else 1\n3845|+\n3846|+\n3847|+if then\n4305|+every ``--period`` seconds prints one JSON object per line with:\n4306|+\n4307|+* the pytest process tree: CPU seconds, working set, threads, page faults;\n4308|+* every notebook kernel (``ipykernel_launcher``) it can see, with the same\n4309|+  counters and its age, so a kernel's CPU seconds can be compared with its wall\n4310|+  seconds (a compute-bound leg tracks wall time; a starved leg does not);\n4311|+* host CPU utilisation, available physical memory, available commit and the\n4312|+  ``Pages Input/sec`` hard-fault counter (``typeperf``), which separates memory\n4313|+  pressure from CPU contention;\n4314|+* the five largest resident processes by name, so resident IDE or language\n4315|+  server load is visible, plus the CPU share of the busiest non-pytest process.\n4316|+\n4317|+It stops when no pytest process has been present for two consecutive samples,\n4318|+and gives up with exit status 3 if none appears within ``--start-grace`` seconds\n4319|+(default 300), so a pytest that exited early or never started cannot leave it\n4320|+looping forever.\n4321|+\n4322|+``--summarize`` reads those lines on standard input and prints, for each\n4323|+notebook-kernel episode, its wall time, CPU seconds, CPU/wall ratio, peak\n4324|+working set, and the host's memory and paging while it ran, plus the pytest\n4325|+tree's working set at the start of the episode.\n4326|+\n4327|+No caller-supplied path is read or written: the sampler writes to standard\n4328|+output and the summariser reads standard input::\n4329|+\n4330|+    .venv/Scripts/python.exe -I EVIDENCE/exp002-d1-s3/suite_sampler.py \\\n4331|+        > samples.jsonl &\n4332|+    ...run the suite...\n4333|+    .venv/Scripts/python.exe -I EVIDENCE/exp002-d1-s3/suite_sampler.py \\\n4334|+        --summarize < samples.jsonl\n4335|+\n4336|+``--timeline`` reads the same lines and prints one row per sample, putting the\n4337|+host's CPU utilisation next to the CPU seconds per second that pytest actually\n4338|+received and the largest other CPU users, which is the F2 correlation table.\n4339|+\n4340|+Windows only (it uses ``GlobalMemoryStatusEx`` and ``typeperf``).\n4341|+\"\"\"\n4342|+\n4343|+from __future__ import annotations\n4344|+\n4345|+import argparse\n4346|+import csv\n4347|+import ctypes\n4348|+import itertools\n4349|+import json\n4350|+import os\n4351|+import subprocess  # nosec B404 - fixed argv, no shell\n4352|+import sys\n4353|+import time\n4354|+from datetime import UTC, datetime\n4355|+from steerable by the caller's environment. system's remaining commit charge headroom in MiB.\n4384|+\n4385|+    Returns:\n4386|+        Available page-file-backed commit in MiB, or ``None`` if unavailable.\n4387|+    \"\"\"\n4388|+    status =   \"\"\"\n4456|+    return any(a == \"-m\" and b == module for a, b in itertools.pairwise(argv))\n4457|+\n4458|+\n4459|+def _counters(procs: list[psutil.Process]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n4460|+
